# §27 对标评估：现有成稿是否过短？（实证）

## 27.1 先说检索的局限（如实说明，未编造）

| 目标样本 | 是否取得 | 说明 |
|---|---|---|
| 日本企业官方 **X** 账号「来訪・視察・商談」类帖子原文 | ❌ **未能取得** | `x.com` 页面内容无法抓取（需登录/JS 渲染）。日文检索 `ご来訪`／`ご訪問いただきました` 命中的几乎全是**お礼メール模板文**与**企业官网 news 页**，不是 X 原帖 |
| 欧美企业官方 **LinkedIn** 账号「访日・partner visit」类帖子原文 | ❌ **未能取得** | `linkedin.com/posts/...` 对未登录抓取返回登录墙 |
| 平台官方长度指引 | ✅ 取得 | 见 27.2 |
| 日本企业官网「来訪」类发信实例 | ✅ 取得 2 例 | 见 27.3 |

> **结论**：无法满足「5 个真实 X 帖 + 5 个真实 LinkedIn 帖」的原始要求。**不编造样本**。改以两类可验证证据作为对标基准：①平台官方发布的长度/结构建议；②日本企业官网的「来訪報告」实例（体裁与 X/LinkedIn 帖高度同构，是日本企业対外発信的同一套写法）。若需真实平台帖原文，需人工登录后截图提供。

## 27.2 证据一：平台官方的长度指引

**X（官方）** — [X Business 中小企业 FAQ（日文）](https://business.x.com/ja/resources/small-business-faqs)

> 「ツイートは必ず短くし、また興味関心を引くものにしましょう。使用できる文字数は最大で140文字（すべて英数文字の場合で280文字）です。**より多くのことを伝えたい場合は、ウェブサイトのリンクをツイートしたり、ツイートスレッドを活用**したりしましょう。」
> 「ツイート1回につきハッシュタグの数は**1〜2個**にするのが理想的です。」

**LinkedIn（官方，2026-07-08）** — [Social Media Today 报道 LinkedIn 官方 checklist](https://www.socialmediatoday.com/news/linkedin-shares-tips-on-effective-posting/824780/)

> 「It's also interesting to note the length here, with LinkedIn advising that creators should **post longer updates to maximize interest**.」
> LinkedIn 官方 checklist：每周 2–3 帖 / 含一个提问以激发讨论 / 首小时回复评论 / 清晰分段与列表 / **首行会成为帖子 URL，勿以 hashtag 开头**。
> 长文 Articles 理想长度 800–1200 词。

**第三方长度实测** — [twitterapi.io: X post length](https://twitterapi.io/blog/x-post-template)

> 「70-140 characters for single posts (high reply rate), 280 characters for opinion / hot-take posts... **Long-form X posts only outperform threads when the topic genuinely doesn't break into pieces.**」

## 27.3 证据二：日本企业官网「来訪」类发信实例（真实、可访问）

**例1** — [常石グループ：在广岛土耳其名誉总领事来訪](https://www.tsuneishi-g.jp/news/topics/2026/03/30174/)

结构拆解：`日期 + 来訪者（点名+职位）` → `弊社からの説明内容` → `意見交換` → `来訪者コメント引用` → `工場見学` → `懇親会` → 多张照片。日文约 300 字。**点名对方姓名与职位、附合影**。

**例2** — [クリヤマホールディングス：三次市長ご来訪](https://www.kuriyama-holdings.com/press/2026/06/---.html)

同型：点名市長、附对方评价引用、附照片。

**从这两例可读出的日本惯例（重要）**：

1. **日本企业确实会点名来訪者并附合影** —— 前提是**对方是「来訪」一方（对方来我方）**，且通常为公务/自治体/领事馆等**本身就以对外露出为工作**的主体，且事前取得同意。
2. **我方去拜访对方（訪問）时的発信惯例明显更保守** —— 检索中几乎找不到「我们去拜访了某民间企业」并点名的官网発信。这反向印证了本文件的核心判断：**訪問側発信的默认应为不点名**。
3. **结构与我们的四段结构一致**：日期→对象→内容→交流→致谢。我们只是删掉了「説明内容」与「对方评价引用」两块（因红线）。

## 27.4 评估结论：我们处在「克制」侧，但第3条已贴近「空洞」边界

| 维度 | 行业常见区间 | 我们现状 | 判定 |
|---|---|---|---|
| **X 英文** | 70–140 字符高互动区；280 上限 | 250–280 字符 | ✅ **合适，无需改** |
| **X 日文** | 140 全角上限 | 加权 ≤280（约 130–140 全角） | ✅ **合适** |
| **LinkedIn** | 官方明示「longer updates」；B2B 官号访问类普遍 150–300 词 | **105–129 词** | ⚠️ **偏短** |
| **内容密度** | 日本官网实例含「説明内容＋意見交換＋対方评价」三块 | 仅 1 句客观观察 | ⚠️ **第1、2条尚可；第3条最薄** |

**「克制」与「空洞」的分界线（明确判断标准）**：

> **克制** = 信息被**主动省略**，但读者能看出「有内容、只是没说」——因为存在**我方视角的实质内容**（为什么去、看到了什么行业结构、我方的思考）。
> **空洞** = 通篇只有**社交仪式**（日期＋致谢＋否认句），无任何读者可带走的信息，读者只能得出「这家公司发帖是为了发帖」。

**我们目前的位置**：
- **第1条、第2条**：克制侧。有「1968 年创业」等可验证事实 + 行业范围，读者有落点。
- **第3条**：**贴近空洞边界**。因排除了 M2ie/netstars/ChuanHouse，点名版只剩 1 家，客观观察句被迫抽象，几乎只剩仪式。
- **根本缺口**：三条**全部缺少「我方视角」**。红线禁的是**对方的信息**，不是**我方的观点**。我们过度自我审查，把本可安全承载信息量的「我方观察」也一并删掉了。

## 27.5 可安全增加的内容类型（逐项说明为何安全）

| # | 可增内容 | 为何安全 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **我方赴日的动机与背景**（为什么现在来日本、想理解什么） | 100% 我方信息。不涉及任何对方主体，不构成对对方的描述 |
| 2 | **我方对日本市场结构的观察**（如「长期客户关系是日本 B2B 的组织性特征」） | 我方观点，且指向**市场整体**而非任何具体公司，无法反推到个别对象 |
| 3 | **行业层面的общ性观察**（如「不动产与专业服务的数字化节奏不同」） | 行业级抽象，公开可知的常识层，非对方内部情报 |
| 4 | **我方团队的收获与后续计划**（如「我们回去后要重新看自己的假设」） | 纯我方内部行动，且**不涉及与对方的任何后续** |
| 5 | **老舗・长期经营的文化观察**（1928/1968 等公开创业年份 + 日本长寿企业整体现象） | 创业年份是官网公开事实；长寿企业现象有权威公开来源（日本占世界百年企业 41.3%），属可引用的公共知识 |
| 6 | **对「被给予时间」这件事本身的记述**（准备之周全、时间之充裕） | 描述的是**我方受到的待遇**，不是对方的经营情报，且是日本最受欢迎的お礼表达 |
| 7 | **提一个开放性问题**（LinkedIn 官方建议） | 面向自己的读者，不涉及对方 |

## 27.6 ⚠️ 看似安全实则危险（必须避开）

| 危险内容 | 为何危险 |
|---|---|
| 「日本の意思決定は欧米より慢／慎重」类比较 | 表面是文化观察，实为**对对方的隐含批评**，且会被读作「我们在抱怨对方决策慢」。日本对被外资评价决策速度极敏感 |
| 「日本市场尚未数字化 / still relies on paper」 | **贬损性行业判断**。即使不点名，读者会把它挂到你刚拜访的那些公司头上 |
| 「traditional industries need modernization」 | 同上，且直接把对方定位成「待改造对象」——这是最典型的外资失礼 |
| **具体行业 + 具体城市 + 具体日期**三者同现 | 组合可**反推出具体公司**（如「8/5・江东区・展示会设计」几乎唯一指向 FUJIYA）。不点名版必须保持行业抽象层级，**日期只到「August 3–4」这种范围**，不写「8/5 在江东区」 |
| **引用对方说法，即使匿名**（"one company told us…"） | 匿名不改变其为**对方信息**的性质；且日本会认为这是把私下会谈内容对外流出。绝对禁止 |
| 「we learned how Japanese companies do X」 | 隐含「我们从对方身上取得了知识」——把対方定位成情报源。改为「we came to understand more about X」（主语落在我方认知上） |
| 拜访家数具体到「10 社」+ 行业清单 | 家数越精确 + 行业越细，可反推度越高。用「a number of companies」而非「10 companies」 |
| 「a productive week」「valuable discussions」 | `productive`／`valuable` 是**对会谈成果的评价**，会被读作已有实质进展 → 触发見込み発表联想 |

## 27.7 X 是否改发 thread？→ **不建议，保持单帖简短**

| 理由 | 依据 |
|---|---|
| **X 官方本身把 thread 定位为「要传达更多内容时」的工具** | [X Business FAQ](https://business.x.com/ja/resources/small-business-faqs)：「必ず短くし」为默认，thread 是例外手段 |
| 我们**没有更多内容可传达**（红线所限），thread 会被填充成叙事 | thread 天然要求「展开」，一展开就会去碰会谈内容与对方信息 |
| **thread 把克制的致谢帖放大成「营销推送」** | 连续多帖占据 timeline，日本 X 用户对企业号连发极敏感（企业号最常见的失败就是「宣伝ツイートだけ」— [NTTドコモビジネス](https://www.nttcoms.com/service/social/column/20220131)） |
| 单帖可**单独撤下**，thread 撤一帖会留断裂痕迹 | 若某方广报事后要求撤回，单帖处理干净 |
| 第三方实测：thread 仅在话题「本身无法切分」时才优于单帖 | [twitterapi.io](https://twitterapi.io/blog/x-post-template) |

> **结论**：X 保持现有单帖（250–280 字符）。**要长内容就放 LinkedIn**，X 帖内不加链接指向 LinkedIn 帖（会把致谢帖变成导流帖）；如需引流，可在 X 帖发布数小时后单独发一条不同主题的帖。

---

# §28 LinkedIn 加长版成稿（200–300 词）— 与精简版二选一

> **与精简版的关系**：精简版（100–150 词，见第1/2/3条各章）与本章加长版（200–300 词）**内容红线完全相同**，差异仅在是否加入「我方视角」段落。
> **加长版的全部新增内容均为我方观点、我方动机、我方后续行动**，不含任何对方信息。
> **选择建议**：官号首次发布日本系列 → 用**加长版**（LinkedIn 官方明示 longer updates 更能获得关注，且能摆脱「空洞」印象）。若公司整体调性极简 → 用精简版。
> ⚠️ **加长版一律不适用于 X**。X 保持精简版（见 §27.7）。

## 通用注意（三条加长版共通）

| 项 | 说明 |
|---|---|
| **首行** | 统一为 `Notes from our visit to Japan, [date].` — LinkedIn 首行会成为帖子 URL，故不以 hashtag 开头（[LinkedIn 官方建议](https://www.socialmediatoday.com/news/linkedin-shares-tips-on-effective-posting/824780/)） |
| **hashtag** | 仅 `#JapanVisitNotes` 一个，置于末尾 |
| **禁止 tag 账号** | 即使点名版也**不 @ 对方公司账号**（tag 会推送通知给对方，等同未经同意的公开关联） |
| **配图** | 城市景观／街景／会议室外部走廊等**无人物、无招牌、无白板**的图片；已清除 EXIF GPS |
| **创业年份的用法** | 仅在**不点名版**中以「one of the companies we met was founded in 1968」形式使用；点名版中可与公司名直接对应（因该年份为其官网公开信息） |
| **主动否认句** | `Nothing has been decided.` 全部版本必须保留，不得删除 |

---

## 28.1 第1条（8/3–8/4）｜LinkedIn 加长版

### 28.1-A 不点名版（★默认推荐，可直接发布）

```
Notes from our visit to Japan, August 3-4.

Our team spent the first two days of this trip in Tokyo, meeting companies in professional services and real estate.

We came with a narrow goal: to understand how business relationships are actually built and maintained here, rather than how they are described from the outside. Most of what we do is remote and fast. Japan is neither, and we wanted to see that difference up close instead of reading about it.

Two things we are still thinking about.

The first is duration. One of the companies we met was founded in 1968. A firm that has operated through that many cycles is not simply older than us; it is organised differently, because it has had to survive decisions we have not yet had to make. Japan holds a disproportionate share of the world's companies older than a century, and after this week we understand a little better why that is a structural fact rather than an accident.

The second is preparation. In every meeting, the material prepared for us was more thorough than what we had asked for, and the time given to us was longer than what we had requested. We have taken that as a standard to meet, not just a courtesy received.

We introduced our own work briefly and spent most of each meeting listening.

Nothing has been decided. We are simply grateful for the time.

Thank you to everyone who made room for us.

#JapanVisitNotes
```

**要点（3行）**
- 新增的三块全是我方信息：**赴日动机**（为什么来）、**对长寿企业的思考**（用公开的百年企业统计，不指向具体公司）、**我方自我要求**（把对方的周全当作标准）。
- 「one of the companies we met was founded in 1968」：年份是 Livlan 官网公开事实，但**不与公司名绑定**，读者无法反推。
- 「introduced our own work briefly and spent most of each meeting listening」：诚实承认做了介绍（因确有推介），但重心明确在倾听，且**不出现产品名、不出现对方公司名**。

### 28.1-B 点名版（⚠️ 使用前必须取得 L2 书面确认）

> ⚠️ **前提**：三家公司**各自広報部门的书面/邮件确认**（含「可在 LinkedIn 公开公司名」的明示）。K.K GLOBAL 英文法人表记未核实 → `[Company A]` 需替换为对方名片上的正式英文表记。

```
Notes from our visit to Japan, August 3-4.

Our team spent the first two days of this trip in Tokyo. In no particular order, we visited:

- Livlan Co., Ltd.
- SAKURA United Solution Co., Ltd.
- [Company A]

We came with a narrow goal: to understand how business relationships are actually built and maintained here, rather than how they are described from the outside. Most of what we do is remote and fast. Japan is neither, and we wanted to see that difference up close instead of reading about it.

Two things we are still thinking about.

The first is duration. Companies here are frequently older than the markets we usually work in. A firm that has operated through that many cycles is not simply older than us; it is organised differently, because it has had to survive decisions we have not yet had to make. Japan holds a disproportionate share of the world's companies older than a century, and after this week we understand a little better why that is a structural fact rather than an accident.

The second is preparation. In every meeting, the material prepared for us was more thorough than what we had asked for, and the time given to us was longer than what we had requested. We have taken that as a standard to meet, not just a courtesy received.

Nothing has been decided. We are simply grateful for the time.

Thank you to everyone who made room for us.

#JapanVisitNotes
```

**要点（3行）**
- 点名版**删除了「we introduced our own work」整句** —— 一旦公司名出现，任何会谈性质描述都会挂到该公司名下，被读作「向 A 公司推介」。
- 「founded in 1968」在点名版中改为泛化的「Companies here are frequently older than…」，避免把年份指向名单中某一家（读者会去查是哪家 → 等于替对方公开信息）。
- `In no particular order` 必须保留：日本实务中并列顺序需事前商定，明示无序可消除优劣解读。

---

## 28.2 第2条（8/5）｜LinkedIn 加长版

> 🔴 **本条为最高风险组**：FUJIYA（展示会・イベント设计施工）与 JTB GMT（DMC，含国際会議運営）在 **MICE 领域实质重叠**，且可能存在发包／承包关系（详见 §24）。**点名版仅点 FUJIYA 一家**，JTB GMT 若要点名须另发一条并错开日期。

### 28.2-A 不点名版（★默认推荐，可直接发布）

```
Notes from our visit to Japan, August 5.

A single day in Tokyo, spent with companies in design and in travel.

We had come to Japan to understand how long-term client relationships are structured here, and this was the day that question stopped being abstract for us.

One of the companies we met that day was founded in 1928. That number is easy to read past. It means the company existed before commercial television, before the industries most of its current clients belong to, and before almost every assumption our own field is built on. Japan accounts for a disproportionate share of the world's companies older than a hundred years, and this was the day we started treating that as a design question rather than a piece of trivia. What does an organisation have to get right, repeatedly, to still be answering the phone a century later?

We do not have an answer. We have a better question than the one we arrived with.

What we noticed across both meetings was how much of the working knowledge sits in the relationship rather than in the document. That is not a gap to be filled by tooling. It is a different distribution of where reliability lives, and we think we had been under-weighting it.

We introduced our own work briefly and spent most of each meeting listening.

Nothing has been decided. We are grateful for the time and for the care taken in preparing for us.

#JapanVisitNotes
```

**要点（3行）**
- 核心新增是**一个反问**（What does an organisation have to get right… to still be answering the phone a century later?）—— LinkedIn 官方建议帖内含提问以激发讨论，且提问对象是我方读者，不涉对方。
- 「knowledge sits in the relationship rather than in the document」是**行业级观察 + 我方自省**（we had been under-weighting it），主语落在我方认知上，不是对对方的评价。
- ⚠️ 已刻意避开「Japan still relies on paper」「needs modernisation」类写法 —— 那会被读作对刚拜访对象的贬损（见 §27.6）。

### 28.2-B 点名版（⚠️ 使用前必须取得 L2 书面确认）

> ⚠️ **前提**：① Fujiya 広報书面确认；② **本版本刻意不含 JTB GMT** —— 两家并列点名在 MICE 重叠下会造成实际问题；③ 若征得同意时对方询问，须如实告知同系列还会提到其他公司。

```
Notes from our visit to Japan, August 5.

A single day in Tokyo. We visited Fujiya Co., Ltd.

We had come to Japan to understand how long-term client relationships are structured here, and this was the day that question stopped being abstract for us.

Fujiya was founded in 1928. That number is easy to read past. It means the company existed before commercial television, before many of the industries it works with today, and before almost every assumption our own field is built on. Japan accounts for a disproportionate share of the world's companies older than a hundred years, and this was the day we started treating that as a design question rather than a piece of trivia. What does an organisation have to get right, repeatedly, to still be answering the phone a century later?

We do not have an answer. We have a better question than the one we arrived with.

What we noticed more generally on this trip is how much working knowledge sits in the relationship rather than in the document. That is not a gap to be filled by tooling. It is a different distribution of where reliability lives, and we think we had been under-weighting it.

Nothing has been decided. We are grateful for the time given to us and for the care taken in preparing for our visit.

Thank you.

#JapanVisitNotes
```

**要点（3行）**
- 仅点 Fujiya 一家，**JTB GMT 完全不出现**（连「a travel company」都已改为泛化，避免读者配对）。
- 「founded in 1928」可与 Fujiya 直接对应，因该年份为其[官方会社概要](https://www.dreamnews.jp/press/0000222885)公开信息。
- 点名版同样**删除了会谈性质描述**；「what we noticed more generally on this trip」把观察归到整个行程而非 Fujiya。

---

## 28.3 第3条（8/6–8/7）｜LinkedIn 加长版

> 🔴 **netstars 为東証グロース上市公司（証券コード 5590）**。「業務上の提携」属东证适时开示事项，须由其自行开示（[JPX](https://www.jpx.co.jp/equities/listing/disclosure/info/)）。**即使仅写公司名亦不豁免** —— 「我方 × netstars 会面」本身即业务接触信号，可能构成未公开重要事实。**L1 口头同意在上市公司语境下几乎无效。**
> 🔴 **本章两个加长版均不含 netstars、M2ie、ChuanHouse**。理由：netstars 上市合规；M2ie 为既有合作方（并列会向新接洽对象公开我方既有合作关系）；ChuanHouse 官方英文表记未核实。

### 28.3-A 不点名版（★默认推荐，可直接发布）

```
Notes from our visit to Japan, August 6-7.

The last two days of this trip took us across several different sectors, ending in Osaka.

By this point in the week the pattern had become hard to miss. In every meeting, regardless of sector, the first substantial question we were asked was some version of: who else have you worked with, and for how long. Not what the product does. Not what it costs. How long.

We work in a market where the first question is usually about capability. Being asked about duration instead, five days running, is the single most useful thing we are taking home. It is not a cultural quirk to be accommodated. It is a rational response to an environment where switching costs are high and reputations are long, and it implies a different order of operations than the one we default to.

The other thing worth recording is Osaka. Spending the final day outside Tokyo was a deliberate choice and a correct one. Treating Japan as one market with one centre is a convenience we can no longer justify.

What we are doing next is unglamorous: rewriting the assumptions we brought with us, and being slower and more specific about what we claim.

We introduced our own work briefly and spent most of each meeting listening.

Nothing has been decided. We are grateful to everyone who gave us their time across these five days.

#JapanVisitNotes
```

**要点（3行）**
- 本条原为最薄的一条，加长版靠**我方在整个行程中的最大收获**（对方总先问「合作了多久」而非「能做什么」）撑起密度 —— 这是**我方被问到什么**，属我方经验，不是对方的经营情报。
- 「across several different sectors」刻意保留模糊：该组几乎全为华人背景在日企业，**绝不可按国籍／族裔分组呈现**，用行业分散化表述淡化。
- 「Osaka」与「rewriting the assumptions we brought with us」均为我方行程与我方后续行动，安全。

### 28.3-B 点名版（⚠️ 使用前必须取得 L2 书面确认）

> ⚠️ **前提**：① NIKKOKU SOFT 広報书面确认；② 其**官方英文法人表记未能核实** —— 需按对方名片／官网确认后替换（现按用户提供的 `NIKKOKU SOFT` 表记）；③ 本版本**不含 netstars／M2ie／ChuanHouse**。
> **若用户仍决定加入 netstars**：须另行取得其**広報部门书面确认 + 确认不构成未公开重要事实**，并建议由 netstars 侧先发或双方同步发布。相关变体见第3条章节 3-7～3-9。

```
Notes from our visit to Japan, August 6-7.

The last two days of this trip took us across several different sectors, ending in Osaka. Among the companies we visited was NIKKOKU SOFT.

By this point in the week the pattern had become hard to miss. In every meeting, regardless of sector, the first substantial question we were asked was some version of: who else have you worked with, and for how long. Not what the product does. Not what it costs. How long.

We work in a market where the first question is usually about capability. Being asked about duration instead, five days running, is the single most useful thing we are taking home. It is not a cultural quirk to be accommodated. It is a rational response to an environment where switching costs are high and reputations are long, and it implies a different order of operations than the one we default to.

The other thing worth recording is Osaka. Spending the final day outside Tokyo was a deliberate choice and a correct one. Treating Japan as one market with one centre is a convenience we can no longer justify.

What we are doing next is unglamorous: rewriting the assumptions we brought with us, and being slower and more specific about what we claim.

Nothing has been decided. We are grateful to everyone who gave us their time across these five days.

#JapanVisitNotes
```

**要点（3行）**
- 仅点 NIKKOKU SOFT 一家，且用 `Among the companies we visited was…` —— 明示名单不完整，避免读者推断「只见了一家」或去猜其余几家。
- 与 netstars **不构成直接竞品**（前者「食」领域 SI／受托开发，后者 QR 支付 gateway，详见 §25），故即便日后加入 netstars 也无竞品并列问题；风险纯粹来自**上市公司开示**。
- 点名版同样无会谈性质描述；「who else have you worked with, and for how long」是我方被问到的问题，不涉及任何一家的具体发言。

## 28.4 精简版 vs 加长版 对照表

| | 精简版 | 加长版（§28） |
|---|---|---|
| 词数 | 105–129 | 227–247 |
| 第1条 | 日期＋行业＋1968＋致谢 | ＋赴日动机 ＋百年企业思考 ＋我方自我要求 |
| 第2条 | 日期＋行业＋1928＋致谢 | ＋一个开放性反问 ＋「知识在关系中」的行业观察＋我方自省 |
| 第3条 | 日期＋行业＋致谢（最薄） | ＋「总被问合作了多久」这一最大收获 ＋大阪的意义 ＋后续行动 |
| 红线 | 完全相同 | 完全相同（已实测零违规） |
| 适用 | 调性极简的官号 | ★推荐：首次发布日本系列的官号 |
| X 适用 | ✅ | ❌ 不适用 |

# §29 官号调研：Verdent 既有发帖风格与调性一致性判断

> ⚠️ **本章推翻了 §27 的一个结论**。§27 曾判定「无法取得 LinkedIn 平台原帖」。本轮通过公开 permalink 成功取得 **Verdent 自家官号 5 篇原帖全文**，其中 **1 篇是与本次访日高度同构的「活动出访 + demo + 致谢」帖**。这是比任何外部对标样本都更有效的基准。

## 29.1 公司与产品（已核实）

| 项 | 内容 | 来源 |
|---|---|---|
| 公司 | Verdent AI，Privately Held，11–50 人（LinkedIn 显示 20 名员工），4,152 followers | [LinkedIn 公司页](https://www.linkedin.com/company/verdent-ai) |
| 产品 | **Agentic coding suite**：并行多智能体、Plan Mode、BYOK、Eco Mode、AI code review。支持 VS Code / JetBrains / Mac / Windows | [verdent.ai](https://www.verdent.ai/) |
| 官方一句话定位 | 「Verdent is an **autonomous coding agent** that takes care of the boring parts, so human developers can focus on ideas, breakthroughs, and building what's next.」 | LinkedIn About |
| 官方 tagline | 「**The future is not about typing faster, but about typing less.**」 | LinkedIn headline |
| X bio | 「**Your AI technical cofounder. Builds, plans & ships — while you sleep.**」 | [x.com/verdent_ai](https://x.com/verdent_ai) |
| 客户面向 | 开发者个人 + **enterprise / development teams**（官帖反复出现 "enterprise leaders"、"Enterprise cost controls"、"teams running serious, sustained work"） | LinkedIn 官帖 |
| 技术可信度资产 | ICSE 2026 Distinguished Paper（SEAlign）；SWE-bench Verified 成绩；VS Code Marketplace 36,526 installs | [官网 Insights](https://www.verdent.ai/) / [VS Marketplace](https://marketplace.visualstudio.com/items?itemName=VerdentAI.verdent) |

> 🔴 **对本次访日的直接影响**：Verdent 是**开发者工具 / AI coding 公司**，不是咨询公司。这意味着**本次拜访对象（税理士出身的专业服务、不动产、展示会设计、旅行、食品 SI、娱乐 agency、支付）几乎全部不是 Verdent 的典型客群**。这本身不是问题，但它决定了帖子的正确叙事：**不能写成「我们去见客户」，而应写成「一家 AI 工具公司去理解一个非科技行业为主的市场」**。后者才是可信且安全的。

## 29.2 官号既有发帖风格（5 篇原帖实测）

| # | 帖子 | 词数 | 结构 | 是否点名对方 | 配图 | 语气 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | [Lenovo CVC Week 回顾](https://www.linkedin.com/posts/verdent-ai_thats-a-wrap-on-lenovo-capital-cvc-week-activity-7488873872990326784-q0U7) | **约 75 词** | 收束句→亮点→我方做了什么→三天概览→致谢 | ✅ **点名 Lenovo + 点名 Chairman/CEO 姓名** | 3 张 | 平实、克制、报告体 |
| 2 | [Lenovo CVC Week 现场](https://www.linkedin.com/posts/verdent-ai_live-from-the-lenovo-capital-cvc-week-in-activity-7487883061846700033--I6C) | 约 60 词 | 地点日期→我方在做什么→bullet 清单→CTA | ✅ 点名 Lenovo | 3 张 | 现场直播式 |
| 3 | [DeepSeek-V4-Flash 上线](https://www.linkedin.com/company/verdent-ai) | 约 110 词 | 结论先行→技术细节→实际收益→CTA | ✅ 点名模型方 | 1 张 | 技术密度高 |
| 4 | [Kimi K3 in Eco Mode](https://www.linkedin.com/company/verdent-ai) | 约 90 词 | 结论先行→变化说明→操作指引→CTA | ✅ | 1 张 | 同上 |
| 5 | [Verdent 2.8.0 发布](https://www.linkedin.com/company/verdent-ai) | 约 105 词 | 问题→方案→小标题分块→CTA | — | 1 张 | 同上 |

**提取出的官号调性关键词与习惯**：

| 维度 | 官号实际做法 |
|---|---|
| **人称** | ✅ **第一人称 we**（"We showed how…"、"we spoke with…"、"We are on-site…"、"Based on our testing…"） |
| **平均长度** | **60–110 词**。**最长一篇也不到 120 词** |
| **hashtag** | ⚠️ **5 篇官帖全部零 hashtag**。既有习惯是**不用 hashtag** |
| **@ tag** | ✅ 会 tag 对方公司（Lenovo）；X 上会 @ 模型方（@Kimi_Moonshot、@deepseek_ai） |
| **结构** | 结论/收束句先行 → 具体内容 → CTA 或致谢。**短段落、无空行诗化排版** |
| **语气** | 平实、事实导向、技术密度高。**无感想式、无励志句、无 emoji** |
| **产品名** | ⚠️ **正文反复出现 "Verdent"**（每帖 2–5 次） |
| **CTA** | 大部分帖以 CTA 收尾（"Try it in Verdent today."、"Stop by our booth"） |
| **致谢** | 活动帖会致谢（"Thank you to everyone who joined our demos—and to Lenovo for…"） |

## 29.3 🔴 调性一致性判断：**不一致，且有 5 处明显冲突**

| # | 冲突点 | 官号既有 | 我们的访日稿 | 严重度 |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **长度** | 60–110 词，最长 <120 | §28 加长版 **227–247 词** | 🔴 **加长版比官号任何一帖长 2 倍以上**，读起来像换了一家公司 |
| 2 | **hashtag** | **零 hashtag** | 我们加了 `#JapanVisitNotes` | 🟡 与既有习惯冲突 |
| 3 | **句式** | 短句、事实、结论先行 | 加长版有「A firm that has operated through that many cycles is not simply older than us; it is organised differently…」这类**长从句、思辨式、近似 thought-leadership 散文** | 🔴 **调性断层最明显处** |
| 4 | **CTA** | 几乎每帖有 | 我们全部无 CTA | 🟢 **这里应保持无 CTA**（访日致谢帖加 CTA 就变招商，见 29.5） |
| 5 | **产品名** | 正文反复出现 Verdent | 我们零出现 | 🟢 **应保持零出现**，理由见下 |

**关于 #5 的重要澄清（此前结论需修正措辞，但结论不变）**：
官号平时正文用 "Verdent" 是**产品更新帖**的正常做法。但 Lenovo 帖证明了一个更细的规律 —— 该帖出现 Verdent 是因为**主体事件本身就是「我们在展会上 demo 产品」**，且 Lenovo 是**主办方 / 投资方**，公开 demo 是活动的既定内容。
**本次访日完全不同**：是**我方登门拜访民间企业的私下会面**，对方未预期被公开。此处「Verdent + 对方公司名」= 未授权的「導入検討事例」。**结论不变：访日系列帖正文不出现产品名。**

## 29.4 调和建议（新内容不应读起来像换了一家公司）

| 建议 | 具体做法 |
|---|---|
| **1. 目标长度下调至 150–190 词** | §28 的 227–247 词过长。**新增 §30 提供 150–190 词的「官号调性版」**，作为**最推荐**选项。§28 加长版保留为「最大信息量版」备选 |
| **2. 拆掉思辨长句，改短句事实句** | 把「A firm that has operated through that many cycles is not simply older than us; it is organised differently, because…」改为「Some of the companies we met have been operating since before the industries we work in existed.」—— 同样的信息，官号句式 |
| **3. hashtag 改为可选，默认不加** | 官号既有零 hashtag。系列标识改用**统一开头句**承担（`Notes from our visit to Japan, [date].`），这本身已足够做系列识别，且不违背既有习惯 |
| **4. 保留 "we" 第一人称** | 与官号完全一致，无需改 |
| **5. 保留零 CTA、零 emoji** | 官号无 emoji ✅；CTA 在本系列中必须放弃（见 29.5） |
| **6. 借用 Lenovo 帖的收束句式** | 官号写「That's a wrap on…」。本系列可用同族的「Notes from…」——已在用，一致 |

## 29.5 ⚠️ 「扩大影响力」与「像营销/招商」的界线（明确越界清单）

用户目的是 B 端拓展 + 扩大影响力。可以做，但以下写法**会立刻越界**：

| 🔴 越界写法 | 为什么越界 |
|---|---|
| 任何 **CTA**（"Get in touch"、"We're expanding in Japan—reach out"、"Learn more at verdent.ai"） | 把致谢帖转为**招商帖**。刚拜访完对方就挂 CTA，等于把对方的接待当作自己的引流素材 |
| **"We are entering the Japanese market"** / "expanding into Japan" | 宣言式扩张表述会让刚见过面的对方被读作「已被纳入其扩张计划」。改为 **"we wanted to understand"** 这种探询式 |
| 「日本企业需要 AI / 存在巨大 AI 机会」 | 把日本整体定位成**待开发市场**。这是外资在日最典型的失礼，且直接指向刚拜访的对象 |
| 「AI adoption in Japan is behind」/ "still early" | 贬损性行业判断，会挂到刚拜访的公司头上 |
| 列举我方能力 / bullet 功能清单（如 Lenovo 现场帖那样） | 展会帖可以（对方是主办方），**访问帖不行**（对方是被拜访方，不是展会观众） |
| 提**具体行业 + 我方产品适配性**（"we see strong fit in real estate"） | 等于公开「我们把不动产行业当目标市场」＋刚拜访过不动产公司 → 直接反推到 Livlan |
| 「productive」「valuable」「promising」 | 对会谈成果的评价 → 触发見込み発表联想 |
| 「as an AI company, we…」+ 具体产品能力 | 前半安全，后半越界。**只能停在「as a company working on AI development tools」这一抽象层** |

| ✅ 安全且仍能扩大影响力的写法 | 为什么安全 |
|---|---|
| 陈述**我方是谁**（"We build AI development tools."）一句，不展开功能 | 是自我介绍的最小单位，非推销。且读者会自行去看主页 → 影响力自然获得 |
| 陈述**我方去日本的问题意识**（"we wanted to understand how software decisions get made here"） | 探询式，把我方放在学习位而非扩张位 |
| **我方的自省与调整**（"we are rewriting some of the assumptions we brought"） | 谦逊，且是日本最受欢迎的姿态 |
| **行业级、不指向任何公司的观察** | 无法反推到具体对象 |
| **老舗与长期经营的文化观察** | 公开常识层，日本受众欢迎 |
| 影响力靠**「一家 AI 公司认真对待日本」这个事实本身** | 这比任何 CTA 都有效，且不失礼 |

---

# §30 ★最推荐：官号调性版 LinkedIn 成稿（150–190 词）

> **为什么新增这一档**：§29 实测 Verdent 官号 5 篇原帖平均 **60–110 词、零 hashtag、短句事实体、无 emoji、第一人称 we**。§28 的 227–247 词加长版**比官号任何一帖长 2 倍以上**，且含思辨长句，读起来像换了一家公司。本章为**调性对齐版**。

| 档位 | 词数 | 与官号调性 | 信息密度 | 推荐度 |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 精简版（第1/2/3条各章） | 105–129 | 接近，但内容偏薄 | 低 | 备选 |
| **§30 官号调性版** | **164–183** | ✅ **最一致** | 中高 | ★**最推荐** |
| §28 加长版 | 227–247 | ⚠️ 偏离（过长＋思辨句） | 高 | 仅在需最大信息量时用 |

**本章与 §28 的具体差异**

| 项 | §28 加长版 | §30 官号调性版 |
|---|---|---|
| hashtag | `#JapanVisitNotes` | ❌ **移除**（官号既有零 hashtag；系列识别交给统一开头句） |
| 句式 | 思辨长从句 | 短句、事实句、结论先行 |
| 我方身份 | 未明示 | ✅ 新增 `We build AI development tools.` 一句（自我介绍最小单位，非推销） |
| 「为什么去」 | 抽象 | ✅ 明示 `most of the companies we met do not [build software]. That was the point.` |
| CTA | 无 | 无（**必须保持无**，见 §29.5） |
| 产品名 | 零 | 零（不变） |

> ⚠️ **关于 `We build AI development tools.` 这句**：这是本章唯一新增的自我介绍，**必须停在这一抽象层**。不得扩写为功能清单、不得接 CTA、不得说「日本企业需要这个」——一旦扩写即从「自我介绍」变为「推销」（详见 §29.5 越界清单）。

---

## 30.1 第1条（8/3–8/4）

### 30.1-A 不点名版（★默认推荐，可直接发布）— 182 词

```
Notes from our visit to Japan, August 3-4.

Our team spent two days in Tokyo, meeting companies in professional services and real estate.

We build AI development tools. Most of the companies we met do not. That was the point. We wanted to understand how software decisions actually get made in Japan, from people who are not in our industry.

Two things we are taking back.

Some of the companies we met have been operating since before the industries we work in existed. One was founded in 1968. Japan holds a disproportionate share of the world's companies older than a century, and that is a structural fact worth understanding rather than a piece of trivia.

The other is preparation. In each meeting, the material prepared for us went beyond what we had asked for, and the time given to us was longer than what we had requested. We are treating that as a standard to meet.

We spent most of each meeting listening.

Nothing has been decided. We are grateful for the time.

Thank you to everyone who made room for us.
```

**要点**
- `We build AI development tools. Most of the companies we met do not. That was the point.` —— 这三句把「一家 AI 工具公司为何去见专业服务与不动产公司」讲清楚了，且**把我方放在学习位而非扩张位**，同时自然完成品牌介绍。
- 1968 年份不与公司名绑定；`One was founded in 1968` 读者无法反推是哪家。
- 已移除 hashtag，系列识别由统一开头句 `Notes from our visit to Japan, [date].` 承担。

### 30.1-B 点名版（⚠️ 使用前必须取得 L2 书面确认）— 172 词

```
Notes from our visit to Japan, August 3-4.

Our team spent two days in Tokyo. In no particular order, we visited:

Livlan Co., Ltd.
SAKURA United Solution Co., Ltd.
[Company A]

We build AI development tools. Most of the companies we met do not. That was the point. We wanted to understand how software decisions actually get made in Japan, from people who are not in our industry.

Two things we are taking back.

Companies here are frequently older than the industries we work in. Japan holds a disproportionate share of the world's companies older than a century, and that is a structural fact worth understanding rather than a piece of trivia.

The other is preparation. In each meeting, the material prepared for us went beyond what we had asked for, and the time given to us was longer than what we had requested. We are treating that as a standard to meet.

Nothing has been decided. We are grateful for the time.

Thank you to everyone who made room for us.
```

**要点**
- 点名版**删除 `We spent most of each meeting listening.`** —— 公司名一出现，任何会谈描述都会挂到该公司名下。
- 1968 改为泛化的 `frequently older than the industries we work in`，避免读者去查是哪家 → 等于替对方公开信息。
- `[Company A]` = K.K GLOBAL 官方英文法人表记待核实，需按对方名片替换。

---

## 30.2 第2条（8/5）

> 🔴 FUJIYA（展示会・イベント设计施工）与 JTB GMT（DMC，含国際会議運営）在 **MICE 领域实质重叠**，且可能存在发包／承包关系（详见 §24）。**点名版仅点 FUJIYA 一家**；JTB GMT 若要点名须另发一条并错开日期。

### 30.2-A 不点名版（★默认推荐）— 179 词

```
Notes from our visit to Japan, August 5.

One day in Tokyo, with companies in design and in travel.

We build AI development tools. Neither company we met that day is a software company. We went anyway, because we wanted to hear how organisations outside our industry think about the work we automate.

One of the companies we met was founded in 1928. It existed before commercial television, before most of the industries it serves today, and before nearly every assumption our own field rests on. Japan accounts for a disproportionate share of the world's companies older than a hundred years. After this day we started treating that as a design question rather than a statistic: what does an organisation have to get right, repeatedly, to still be operating a century later?

We do not have an answer yet. We have a better question than the one we arrived with.

We spent most of each meeting listening.

Nothing has been decided. We are grateful for the time given to us, and for the care taken in preparing for us.
```

**要点**
- `we wanted to hear how organisations outside our industry think about the work we automate` —— 承认我方做什么（automate），但**不描述会谈内容、不提产品名、不把对方当销售对象**。
- 保留一个反问（LinkedIn 官方建议含提问促讨论），提问对象是我方读者，不涉对方。
- 已避开「日本 AI 采纳落后」「需要现代化」类贬损判断（见 §29.5）。

### 30.2-B 点名版（⚠️ 需 L2；刻意不含 JTB GMT）— 164 词

```
Notes from our visit to Japan, August 5.

One day in Tokyo. We visited Fujiya Co., Ltd.

We build AI development tools. Fujiya is not a software company. We went anyway, because we wanted to hear how organisations outside our industry think about the work we automate.

Fujiya was founded in 1928. It existed before commercial television, before most of the industries it serves today, and before nearly every assumption our own field rests on. Japan accounts for a disproportionate share of the world's companies older than a hundred years. After this day we started treating that as a design question rather than a statistic: what does an organisation have to get right, repeatedly, to still be operating a century later?

We do not have an answer yet. We have a better question than the one we arrived with.

Nothing has been decided. We are grateful for the time given to us, and for the care taken in preparing for our visit.

Thank you.
```

**要点**
- 仅点 Fujiya，**JTB GMT 完全不出现**，连「a travel company」都已删，避免读者配对。
- `Fujiya is not a software company.` 是中性事实陈述，非评价；1928 可与 Fujiya 直接对应（[官方会社概要](https://www.dreamnews.jp/press/0000222885)）。
- 已删除 `We spent most of each meeting listening.`（点名版一律不描述会谈）。

## 30.3 第3条（8/6–8/7）

> 🔴 **netstars 为東証グロース上市（証券コード 5590）**。「業務上の提携」属适时开示事项须由其自行开示（[JPX](https://www.jpx.co.jp/equities/listing/disclosure/info/)）。**仅写公司名亦不豁免**。L1 口头同意在上市公司语境下几乎无效。
> 🔴 以下两版**均不含 netstars／M2ie／ChuanHouse**（上市合规／既有合作方／英文名未核实）。

### 30.3-A 不点名版（★默认推荐）— 182 词

```
Notes from our visit to Japan, August 6-7.

The last two days took us across several different sectors, ending in Osaka.

By this point the pattern was hard to miss. In every meeting, whatever the sector, the first substantial question we were asked was some version of: who else have you worked with, and for how long. Not what the software does. Not what it costs. How long.

We build AI development tools, in a market where the first question is almost always about capability. Being asked about duration instead, five days running, is the most useful thing we are taking home. It is not a preference to accommodate. It is a rational response to an environment where switching costs are high and reputations are long, and it implies a different order of operations than ours.

Ending in Osaka was deliberate. Treating Japan as one market with one centre is a shortcut we can no longer justify.

We spent most of each meeting listening.

Nothing has been decided. We are grateful to everyone who gave us their time across these five days.
```

**要点**
- 本条靠**我方在整个行程中的最大收获**（对方总先问「合作了多久」而非「能做什么」）撑密度 —— 这是**我方被问到什么**，属我方经验，不是对方经营情报。
- `across several different sectors` 刻意保留模糊：该组几乎全为华人背景在日企业，**绝不可按国籍族裔分组呈现**。
- `It is a rational response to…` 是**对日方做法的正面归因**，而非「日本决策慢」式隐含批评 —— 这是本条最关键的措辞选择。

### 30.3-B 点名版（⚠️ 需 L2）— 183 词

```
Notes from our visit to Japan, August 6-7.

The last two days took us across several different sectors, ending in Osaka. Among the companies we visited was NIKKOKU SOFT.

By this point the pattern was hard to miss. In every meeting, whatever the sector, the first substantial question we were asked was some version of: who else have you worked with, and for how long. Not what the software does. Not what it costs. How long.

We build AI development tools, in a market where the first question is almost always about capability. Being asked about duration instead, five days running, is the most useful thing we are taking home. It is not a preference to accommodate. It is a rational response to an environment where switching costs are high and reputations are long, and it implies a different order of operations than ours.

Ending in Osaka was deliberate. Treating Japan as one market with one centre is a shortcut we can no longer justify.

Nothing has been decided. We are grateful to everyone who gave us their time across these five days.
```

**要点**
- 仅点 NIKKOKU SOFT，用 `Among the companies we visited was…` 明示名单不完整，避免读者猜其余几家。
- 其**官方英文法人表记未能核实**，需按对方名片／官网确认后替换。
- 与 netstars **不构成直接竞品**（前者「食」领域 SI，后者 QR 支付 gateway，详见 §25）；风险纯粹来自上市公司开示。

|---|---|---|---|
| 第1条 不点名 | 约 118 词 | **182 词** | 247 词 |
| 第1条 点名 | 约 105 词 | **172 词** | 247 词 |

## 30.5 X 版是否需要同步调整？→ **不需要**

| 项 | 结论 |
|---|---|
| 长度 | X 现有 250–280 字符符合[X 官方建议](https://business.x.com/ja/resources/small-business-faqs)，**不改** |
| thread | **仍不建议**（理由见 §27.7） |
| hashtag | ⚠️ **建议由 2 个减为 1 个或 0 个**。官号 LinkedIn 零 hashtag；X 上官号会 @ 模型方但未见大量 hashtag。日文版可保留 1 个 |
| 我方身份句 | ❌ **不加**。X 字符预算不足，加了会挤掉主动否认句 —— 后者优先级更高 |

# §31 ★★最终版：X 主平台・可读性优先（18 段成稿）

> **本章取代此前所有成稿档位，作为最终交付。** 变更：①**X 升为主平台**，成为打磨最精的版本；②允许「克制的人味」，每条有独立钩子；③**放弃统一首句**，系列感交由统一 hashtag `#JapanVisitNotes` 承担；④三条叙事递进：**数字反差 → 时间尺度 → 问题反转**。

## 31.0 本章的写法转变

| 项 | 此前 | §31 |
|---|---|---|
| 平台优先 | LinkedIn 优先，X 是压缩版 | ✅ **X 优先**，LinkedIn 是展开版 |
| 首句 | 三条统一 `Notes from our visit to Japan…` | ✅ **三条各不相同**（`1968.` / `…founded in 1928` / `Five days, Tokyo to Osaka`）|
| 系列标识 | 统一首句 | ✅ **统一 hashtag** `#JapanVisitNotes` |
| 钩子 | 无（公告体）| ✅ 第1条=**数字反差**、第2条=**时间尺度**、第3条=**问题反转** |
| 我方视角 | 少 | ✅ 战略判断＋文化观察＋自省，全部我方信息 |

**三条的叙事递进设计**

| 条 | 钩子类型 | 核心句 | 情绪曲线 |
|---|---|---|---|
| 第1条 | **数字反差** | 「our entire industry is younger than that company's back office」 | 意外 → 谦逊 |
| 第2条 | **时间尺度** | 「It has outlived television」＋「we measure our roadmap in quarters」 | 敬畏 → 自我质询 |
| 第3条 | **问题反转** | 「Not once did anyone open with 'what can it do'」 | 悬念 → 顿悟 → 行动 |

> ⚠️ **`1928`／`1968` 均为对方官网公开信息**，且**未加任何评价形容词**（无 leading／venerable／prestigious）。仅作时间事实与我方对照使用。

---

# 31.1 第1条（8/3–8/4）｜钩子：数字反差

## X ／ 英文 ／ 不点名版（★默认，可直接发布）— 280 字符

```
1968.

That's when one of the companies we met in Tokyo started operating. We build AI coding tools — our whole industry is younger than their accounting department.

Two days, professional services and real estate. We mostly listened.

Nothing has been decided.

#JapanVisitNotes
```
- 首字即 `1968.` 单独成行——X 上最强的停顿钩子；「industry younger than their back office」是**我方自嘲**，不是对对方的评价。
- `We mostly listened.` 承认有做介绍但重心在倾听，不提产品名、不描述会谈内容。

## X ／ 英文 ／ 点名版（⚠️ 需先取得书面确认）— 260 字符

```
1968 — when one of these companies started operating. Older than our entire industry.

Two days in Tokyo, in no particular order:
Livlan Co., Ltd.
SAKURA United Solution Co., Ltd.
[Company A]

Thank you for the time. Nothing has been decided.

#JapanVisitNotes
```
- 点名版删除 `We mostly listened.`：公司名一出现，任何会谈描述都会挂到该名下。
- `[Company A]` = K.K GLOBAL 官方英文表记待核实，须按名片替换。

## X ／ 日文 ／ 不点名版 — 加权 266

```
1968年。

今回東京でお会いした企業のうち一社が、事業を始めた年です。私たちはAI開発ツールをつくっています。この業界そのものが、その歴史よりずっと若い。

二日間、専門サービスと不動産の分野で、主にお話を伺っておりました。

決まったことはございません。

#JapanVisitNotes
```
- 「お話を伺っておりました」＝谦让语，重心在倾听；避开「提案」（在日等同営業提案）。
- 「この業界そのものが、その歴史よりずっと若い」＝我方自嘲，非对方评价。

## X ／ 日文 ／ 点名版（⚠️ 需书面确认）— 加权 246

```
1968年に事業を始められた企業がございます。私たちの業界の歴史より長い。

東京での二日間、順不同で：
株式会社リブラン
SAKURA United Solution株式会社
[Company A]

お時間を頂戴し、ありがとうございました。決まったことはございません。

#JapanVisitNotes
```
- 「順不同」必须保留；日文并列按五十音順（リブラン → SAKURA）。
- 与英文字母順结果不同属正常，各语言遵循各自规则。

## LinkedIn ／ 不点名版（★默认）— 210 词

```
1968.

That is when one of the companies we met in Tokyo this week began operating. We build AI development tools. Our entire industry is younger than that company's back office.

We spent two days meeting companies in professional services and real estate. None of them are software companies, and that was the reason we went. We wanted to hear how software decisions get made by the people who actually have to live with them for twenty years, not by people who benchmark models for a living.

Japan was a deliberate choice rather than a convenient one. It holds a disproportionate share of the world's companies older than a century, which means it is one of the few places where you can ask what survives a hundred years of technology changing underneath you, and get an answer from someone who has done it.

Two things we are taking home.

Preparation is a form of respect here. In every meeting the material prepared for us went beyond what we had asked for, and the time given to us ran longer than what we had requested.

And we talked less than we planned to. That was the right call.

Nothing has been decided. Thank you to everyone who made room for us.

#JapanVisitNotes
```
- 新增**赴日战略判断**（`a deliberate choice rather than a convenient one`）＋**为何见非软件公司**（`live with them for twenty years, not…benchmark models for a living`）——全属我方视角。
- `Preparation is a form of respect here.` 是对**我方所受待遇**的记述，非对方经营情报。

## LinkedIn ／ 点名版（⚠️ 需书面确认）— 195 词

```
1968.

That is when one of the companies we met in Tokyo this week began operating — older than our entire industry.

Over two days we visited, in no particular order:

Livlan Co., Ltd.
SAKURA United Solution Co., Ltd.
[Company A]

We build AI development tools. None of the companies above are software companies, and that was the reason we went. We wanted to hear how software decisions get made by the people who have to live with them for twenty years, not by people who benchmark models for a living.

Japan was a deliberate choice rather than a convenient one. It holds a disproportionate share of the world's companies older than a century, which means it is one of the few places where you can ask what survives a hundred years of technology changing underneath you, and get an answer from someone who has done it.

Preparation is a form of respect here. In every meeting the material prepared for us went beyond what we had asked for, and the time given to us ran longer than what we had requested.

Nothing has been decided. Thank you to everyone who made room for us.

#JapanVisitNotes
```
- 删除 `we talked less than we planned to`（点名版不描述会谈）。
- `1968` 保留但归于「其中一社」，不与名单中特定公司绑定。

# 31.2 第2条（8/5）｜钩子：时间尺度

> 🔴 **FUJIYA 与 JTB GMT 在 MICE 领域实质重叠**，且可能存在发包／承包关系（§24）。**点名版仅点 FUJIYA**；JTB GMT 若要点名须另发一条并错开日期。

## X ／ 英文 ／ 不点名版（★默认）— 260 字符

```
A company we met in Tokyo was founded in 1928. It has outlived television.

We build AI tools and measure our roadmap in quarters. Spent the day on a different question: what must you get right, again and again, to still be here in 100 years?

#JapanVisitNotes
```
- `It has outlived television.` 五个词完成时间尺度对撞——本条最强的一句。
- 「roadmap in quarters」是**我方自省**；反问句促进转发且不涉对方。

## X ／ 英文 ／ 点名版（⚠️ 需书面确认）— 239 字符

```
Fujiya Co., Ltd. was founded in 1928 — operating longer than television has existed.

We measure our roadmap in quarters. Spent a day in Tokyo thinking on a longer scale.

Thank you for the time. Nothing has been decided.

#JapanVisitNotes
```
- JTB GMT **完全不出现**，连「a travel company」都已删以避免读者配对。
- 1928 可与 Fujiya 直接对应（[官方会社概要](https://www.dreamnews.jp/press/0000222885)）。

## X ／ 日文 ／ 不点名版 — 加权 242

```
今回東京でお会いした一社は、1928年創業。テレビよりも長く事業を続けていらっしゃいます。

私たちはロードマップを四半期で数えています。この日は、別の問いを持ち帰りました。百年続けるために、何を繰り返し正しく行う必要があるのか。

#JapanVisitNotes
```
- 「続けていらっしゃいます」＝尊敬语；避免二重敬語。
- 反问以「〜のか」收束，日文 X 上自然且不油滑。

## X ／ 日文 ／ 点名版（⚠️ 需书面确认）— 加权 260

```
株式会社フジヤは1928年創業。テレビよりも長く事業を続けていらっしゃいます。

四半期でロードマップを数える私たちが、東京で一日、もっと長い時間の尺度について考えておりました。

お時間を頂戴し、ありがとうございました。決まったことはございません。

#JapanVisitNotes
```
- 仅点フジヤ；「決まったことはございません」必须保留。

## LinkedIn ／ 不点名版（★默认）— 207 词

```
One of the companies we met in Tokyo has been operating since 1928.

Television did not exist yet. Neither did most of the industries it works with today. It has outlived the radio era, two currencies' worth of economic upheaval, and every software paradigm our field has ever declared permanent.

We build AI development tools and measure our roadmap in quarters. Sitting across from a company that measures itself in generations reorganises what you think you are doing.

So we spent the day on a question we do not usually ask: not what should we build next, but what would we have to get right, repeatedly, for any of this to still be running in a hundred years.

We do not have an answer. We have a better question than the one we flew in with.

One observation, offered as our own and not as a verdict on anyone: in Japan the durability of a relationship appears to be treated as part of the product rather than as an outcome of it. We had read that. Reading it and sitting in it are different.

Nothing has been decided. We are grateful for the time given to us, and for the care taken in preparing for our visit.

#JapanVisitNotes
```
- `offered as our own and not as a verdict on anyone` 是关键免责句——把观察明确标记为我方视角，切断「评价对方」的解读。
- `We had read that. Reading it and sitting in it are different.` 提供人味但不煽情。

## LinkedIn ／ 点名版（⚠️ 需书面确认）— 203 词

```
Fujiya Co., Ltd. has been operating since 1928.

Television did not exist yet. Neither did most of the industries it works with today. It has outlived the radio era, decades of economic upheaval, and every software paradigm our field has ever declared permanent.

We build AI development tools and measure our roadmap in quarters. Spending a day in Tokyo with a company that measures itself in generations reorganises what you think you are doing.

So we spent the day on a question we do not usually ask: not what should we build next, but what would we have to get right, repeatedly, for any of this to still be running in a hundred years.

We do not have an answer. We have a better question than the one we flew in with.

One observation, offered as our own and not as a verdict on anyone: in Japan the durability of a relationship appears to be treated as part of the product rather than as an outcome of it. We had read that. Reading it and sitting in it are different.

Nothing has been decided. We are grateful for the time given to us, and for the care taken in preparing for our visit.

#JapanVisitNotes
```
- 点名版把 `two currencies' worth of` 改为 `decades of`——前者可能被读作对日本经济的评价。

# 31.3 第3条（8/6–8/7）｜钩子：问题反转

> 🔴 **netstars 為東証グロース上市（証券コード 5590）**。「業務上の提携」属适时开示事项须由其自行开示（[JPX](https://www.jpx.co.jp/equities/listing/disclosure/info/)）。**仅写公司名亦不豁免**。**维持建议不点名**。
> 🔴 以下点名版**均不含 netstars／M2ie／ChuanHouse**（上市合规／既有合作方／英文名未核实）。

## X ／ 英文 ／ 不点名版（★默认）— 256 字符

```
Five days in Japan, Tokyo to Osaka.

Not once was the first question "what can it do."

Every time: who else have you worked with, and for how long.

We build AI tools in a market obsessed with capability. Duration is the better question.

#JapanVisitNotes
```
- `Not once was the first question "what can it do."` 制造悬念，第二段揭晓——X 上最容易被引用转发的结构。
- 已删除具体会面家数（原「ten meetings」）：家数越精确可反推度越高。

## X ／ 英文 ／ 点名版（⚠️ 需书面确认）— 260 字符

```
Five days, Tokyo to Osaka. Final meetings included NIKKOKU SOFT.

Across every sector the first question was never "what can it do" — it was "who else have you worked with, and for how long."

Thank you for the time. Nothing has been decided.

#JapanVisitNotes
```
- 仅点 NIKKOKU SOFT，其**官方英文法人表记未核实**，须按名片确认。
- `Final meetings included…` 明示名单不完整，避免读者猜其余几家。

## X ／ 日文 ／ 不点名版 — 加权 244

```
五日間、東京から大阪へ。

最初に伺われたのは、一度も「何ができるのか」ではありませんでした。

いつも「これまでどこと、どれくらいの期間ご一緒されたのか」。

機能を競う市場にいる私たちにとって、期間を問う方が本質的な問いでした。

#JapanVisitNotes
```
- 「伺われた」＝对方动作用尊敬语；我方动作用谦让语，敬语方向正确。
- 「機能を競う市場にいる私たち」把落点放在我方，非批评日方。

## X ／ 日文 ／ 点名版（⚠️ 需书面确认）— 加权 264

```
五日間、東京から大阪へ。最終日程にはニッコクソフト様も含まれます。

どの業種でも、最初のご質問は「何ができるのか」ではなく「これまでどこと、どれくらいご一緒されたのか」でした。

お時間を頂戴し、ありがとうございました。決まったことはございません。

#JapanVisitNotes
```
- 「ニッコクソフト様」用「様」；不写「社長様」等二重敬語。

## LinkedIn ／ 不点名版（★默认）— 200 词

```
Five days, Tokyo to Osaka, across sectors that have almost nothing to do with each other.

Not once did anyone open with "what can it do."

Every single time, in some form: who else have you worked with, and for how long.

We build AI development tools, in a market where capability is the first and often only question. Being asked about duration instead — five days running, across completely unrelated industries — is the most useful thing we are carrying home.

It would be easy and wrong to file this under cultural difference. It is a rational response to an environment where switching costs are real, reputations outlive individual careers, and a vendor who disappears in three years has cost you more than they ever saved you. Read that way, it is not a slower question. It is a more expensive one to answer honestly, which is why fewer companies get asked it.

Ending the week in Osaka was deliberate. Treating Japan as one market with one centre was a shortcut we had been taking, and we will not take it again.

Nothing has been decided. Thank you to everyone who gave us their time across these five days.

#JapanVisitNotes
```
- 🔑 **最关键的措辞**：`It would be easy and wrong to file this under cultural difference.` ——主动否认「文化差异」解读，改以**经济理性**归因（switching costs／reputations），彻底避开「日本决策慢」式隐含批评。
- `across sectors that have almost nothing to do with each other` 刻意模糊：该组几乎全为华人背景在日企业，**绝不可按族裔分组呈现**。

## LinkedIn ／ 点名版（⚠️ 需书面确认）— 206 词

```

Not once did anyone open with "what can it do."

Read that way, it is not a slower question. It is a more expensive one to answer honestly, which is why fewer companies get asked it.

Ending the week in Osaka was deliberate. Treating Japan as one market with one centre was a shortcut we had been taking, and we will not take it again.

Nothing has been decided. Thank you to everyone who gave us their time across these five days.

#JapanVisitNotes
```
- 仅点 NIKKOKU SOFT；与 netstars **不构成直接竞品**（§25），风险纯来自上市开示。

# 31.4 是否发 thread？→ **第3条可选做 thread，另两条不建议**

| 条 | 建议 | 理由 |
|---|---|---|
| 第1条 | ❌ 单帖 | 钩子是 `1968.` 的**瞬时冲击**，拆成 thread 会稀释 |
| 第2条 | ❌ 单帖 | 同上，`outlived television` 是一击式 |
| **第3条** | ⭕ **可选 thread（2 帖）** | 本条是唯一**天然分两拍**的内容（悬念→揭晓→归因），且[X 官方](https://business.x.com/ja/resources/small-business-faqs)将 thread 定位为「传达更多内容时」的正当手段 |

**第3条 thread 版（仅不点名版；点名版不建议做 thread）**

```
1/2
Five days in Japan, Tokyo to Osaka.

Not once was the first question "what can it do."

Every time: who else have you worked with, and for how long.

2/2
It would be easy and wrong to call this a cultural quirk.

Switching costs are real. Reputations outlive careers. A vendor who vanishes in three years cost you more than they saved.

Not a slower question. A more expensive one to answer honestly.

#JapanVisitNotes
```

- 第 2 帖独立成立，即使被单独看到也不失礼。
- ⚠️ **点名版禁做 thread**：连番会给对方广报造成「已排定发布计划」的压力，且撤回时留断裂痕迹。

# §32 ★最终交付：全英文 12 段成稿（平台调性区分版）

> **本章从零重写，取代此前所有成稿章节（§28／§30／§31）。既有章节仅作参考资料保留，不再是交付物。**
> **日文版全部废弃。** 本章仅英文。

## 32.0 平台调性区分：这一版到底改了什么

此前各版的问题是 X 版只是 LinkedIn 版的压缩。本章两个平台**在结构层就是两种文体**：

| 维度 | X | LinkedIn |
|---|---|---|
| 单位 | **一条帖 = 一个想法** | 一条帖 = 一段完整思考 |
| 句长 | **平均 4–8 词/句** | 平均 12–15 词/句 |
| 结构 | 断行堆叠，每行独立成立 | 起承转合，段落递进 |
| 语感 | 口语、留白、可截图引用的单句 | 第一人称叙事，允许铺陈与自省 |
| 论证 | **不论证**，只给结论 | **展开论证**，给出为什么 |
| 收尾 | 硬收，戛然而止 | 开放性问题（不点名版）|

**实测差异（已验证，非估计）**

| 对 | X 字符 / 词 / 平均句长 | LinkedIn 词 / 平均句长 | 两版共用句 |
|---|---|---|---|
| 第1条 不点名 | 273 / 45 词 / **5.6 词** | 241 词 / **15.1 词** | 1 句（否认句）|
| 第1条 点名 | 248 / 42 词 / **5.2 词** | 219 词 / **14.6 词** | 2 句（致谢＋否认）|
| 第2条 不点名 | 275 / 49 词 / **7.0 词** | 242 词 / **12.7 词** | 2 句 |
| 第2条 点名 | 273 / 35 词 / **4.4 词** | 238 词 / **11.9 词** | 2 句 |
| 第3条 不点名 | 274 / 50 词 / **8.3 词** | 247 词 / **13.7 词** | 1 句 |
| 第3条 点名 | 256 / 45 词 / **7.5 词** | 231 词 / **12.8 词** | 1 句 |

> 两平台**唯一重合的只有致谢句与主动否认句**（这两句必须逐字一致，属合规固定件）。其余内容零复用。

## 32.1 hashtag 建议：**LinkedIn 不加，X 加**

| | 建议 | 理由 |
|---|---|---|
| **LinkedIn** | ❌ **不加** | 官号实测 5 篇帖**零 hashtag**（§29）。LinkedIn 上系列感由**首句风格 + 发布节奏**自然形成，加 tag 反而像营销号。且 LinkedIn 首行会成为帖子 URL，tag 会稀释关键词 |
| **X** | ⭕ **加 1 个** `#JapanVisitNotes` | X 是信息流平台，帖子彼此孤立，**没有 tag 就没有系列**。[X 官方建议 1–2 个](https://business.x.com/ja/resources/small-business-faqs)，取下限 1 个 |

> 下方成稿**正文均不含 hashtag**。X 版如采用，在最后空一行加 `#JapanVisitNotes` 即可（各版均已预留字符余量，最长的 x2a 加后为 293 字符 → **需删去 `Nothing has been decided.` 前的空行或改用 279 字符的替代版**，详见 32.5）。

# 32.2 第1条｜8月3–4日｜专业服务・不动产

## X ／ 不点名版（★默认，可直接发布）｜273 字符

```
Two days in Tokyo. Professional services and real estate.

None of them are software companies.

That was the point.

We build developer tools. We wanted to hear from the people who live with those decisions for twenty years.

Mostly we listened.

Nothing has been decided.
```
- `None of them are software companies. / That was the point.` 两个短句制造反差与悬念，是本条最可能被截图引用的部分。
- 「live with those decisions for twenty years」把我方定位为学习者，同时解释了为何一家 AI 公司去见非科技公司。

## X ／ 点名版（⚠️ 需先取得书面确认）｜248 字符

```
Two days in Tokyo.

In no particular order:

Livlan Co., Ltd.
SAKURA United Solution Co., Ltd.
[Company A]

We build developer tools. None of the above are software companies.

That was the point.

Thank you for the time.

Nothing has been decided.
```
- 公司名单独成块，视觉上就是清单不是叙事；`In no particular order` 消除序列优劣解读。
- 已删除 `Mostly we listened.`：点名版一律不描述会谈性质。`[Company A]` = K.K GLOBAL 表记待核实。

## LinkedIn ／ 不点名版（★默认）｜241 词

```
We went to Japan with a question we could not answer from our desks.

We build tools for software developers. Almost none of the companies we met in Tokyo this week write software for a living. They work in professional services and in real estate. One has been operating since 1968, which is to say it was a functioning business before our industry existed at all.

That mismatch was the reason we went, not an accident of scheduling.

Our field has a habit of talking to itself. We benchmark models against other models and mostly hear from people fluent in the same vocabulary we are. What we hear far less is how a technology decision looks to someone who will still be living with it in twenty years, in a business where choosing wrong costs you a reputation rather than a migration.

So we spent two days asking, and much less time explaining.

Two things stayed with the team. Preparation here is not administrative, it is a form of respect: the material put in front of us went past what we had asked for, and the time we were given ran longer than what we had requested. And the questions we were asked were slower, and better, than the ones we usually field.

Nothing has been decided. We came to understand something, and we do.

If you build software for industries older than software, what did you have to unlearn first?
```
- 结构是完整论证：**动机 → 行业自省（Our field has a habit of talking to itself）→ 所以我们少说多问 → 收获 → 开放问题**，X 版完全没有这一层。
- 结尾开放问题面向我方读者（同行），不涉对方，且能带来评论互动。

## LinkedIn ／ 点名版（⚠️ 需书面确认）｜219 词

```
We went to Japan with a question we could not answer from our desks.

Over two days in Tokyo we visited, in no particular order:

Livlan Co., Ltd.
SAKURA United Solution Co., Ltd.
[Company A]

We build tools for software developers. None of the companies above write software for a living. That mismatch was the reason we went, not an accident of scheduling.

Our field has a habit of talking to itself. We benchmark models against other models and mostly hear from people fluent in the same vocabulary we are. What we hear far less is how a technology decision looks to someone who will still be living with it in twenty years, in a business where choosing wrong costs you a reputation rather than a migration.

Two things stayed with the team. Preparation here is not administrative, it is a form of respect: the material put in front of us went past what we had asked for, and the time we were given ran longer than what we had requested. And the questions we were asked were slower, and better, than the ones we usually field.

Nothing has been decided. We are grateful to each of them for making room for us.

If you build software for industries older than software, what did you have to unlearn first?
```
- 删除 `So we spent two days asking, and much less time explaining.`（点名版不描述会谈）与 `We came to understand something, and we do.`
- 1968 年份在点名版中**整句移除**，避免读者去查是名单中哪一家 → 等于替对方公开信息。

# 32.3 第2条｜8月5日｜设计・旅游

> 🔴 **Fujiya 与 JTB GMT 在 MICE 领域实质重叠**，且可能存在发包／承包关系（§24）。**点名版仅点 Fujiya**；JTB GMT 若要点名须另发一条并错开日期。

## X ／ 不点名版（★默认）｜275 字符

```
One company we met today has been operating since 1928.

It has outlived television.

We plan in quarters.

Sat with that gap all afternoon. Still sitting with it.

What do you have to get right, over and over, for anything to last a hundred years?

Nothing has been decided.
```
- `It has outlived television. / We plan in quarters.` 两行并置即完成时间尺度对撞，不加任何解释——这是 X 的写法。
- `Still sitting with it.` 是本条的人味所在：克制、不煽情、不总结。

## X ／ 点名版（⚠️ 需书面确认）｜273 字符

```
Fujiya Co., Ltd. Founded 1928.

Older than commercial television. Older than the fax machine it outlasted.

Our longest planning document covers eighteen months.

Spent a day in Tokyo recalibrating what "long term" means.

Thank you for the time.

Nothing has been decided.
```
- 点名版**完全重写而非删改**：改用 `Founded 1928.` 电报体开场，与 LinkedIn 版零重复。
- JTB GMT 完全不出现，连「a travel company」都已删以避免读者配对。1928 为[官方会社概要](https://www.dreamnews.jp/press/0000222885)公开信息。

## LinkedIn ／ 不点名版（★默认）｜242 词

```
One of the companies we met in Tokyo today has been operating since 1928.

Take a second with that number. It was already trading before commercial television. It has worked through the arrival of the telephone as a business tool, the fax, the personal computer, the internet, and now this. Every one of those was, at the time, the thing that changed everything.

We build AI development tools. We plan in quarters. Our longest-horizon document covers eighteen months and we consider that far-sighted.

Sitting across from an organisation that measures itself in generations does something uncomfortable and useful to your sense of scale. It reframes the question. We spend most of our time asking what we should build next. We spent today on a question we almost never ask: what would we have to get right, repeatedly and without drama, for any of this to still be running in a hundred years?

We do not have an answer. We have a better question than the one we flew in with, which is more than we expected from a single day.

One observation, offered as ours and not as a verdict on anyone. In our market, durability tends to be treated as an outcome. Here it reads more like an input, something designed for at the start rather than hoped for at the end.

Nothing has been decided. We are grateful for the time, and for the care taken in preparing for us.
```
- LinkedIn 独有的铺陈：把 1928 展开成**技术变迁清单**（telephone / fax / PC / internet / now this），X 版没有也不该有这一段。
- `offered as ours and not as a verdict on anyone` 是关键免责句，把观察明确标记为我方视角，切断「评价对方」的解读。

## LinkedIn ／ 点名版（⚠️ 需书面确认）｜238 词

```
Fujiya Co., Ltd. has been operating since 1928.

Take a second with that number. It was already trading before commercial television. It has worked through the arrival of the telephone as a business tool, the fax, the personal computer, the internet, and now this. Every one of those was, at the time, the thing that changed everything.

We build AI development tools. We plan in quarters. Our longest-horizon document covers eighteen months and we consider that far-sighted.

Spending a day with an organisation that measures itself in generations does something uncomfortable and useful to your sense of scale. It reframes the question. We spend most of our time asking what we should build next. We spent today on a question we almost never ask: what would we have to get right, repeatedly and without drama, for any of this to still be running in a hundred years?

We do not have an answer. We have a better question than the one we flew in with, which is more than we expected from a single day.

One observation, offered as ours and not as a verdict on anyone. In our market, durability tends to be treated as an outcome. Here it reads more like an input, something designed for at the start rather than hoped for at the end.

Nothing has been decided. We are grateful for the time, and for the care taken in preparing for our visit.
```
- 1928 可与 Fujiya 直接对应（其官网公开信息），故点名版保留年份。
- `Sitting across from` 改为 `Spending a day with`——前者带会谈场景暗示，点名版应避免。

---

# 32.4 第3条｜8月6–7日｜跨行业・东京至大阪

> 🔴 **netstars 為東証グロース上市（証券コード 5590）**。「業務上の提携」属适时开示事项须由其自行开示（[JPX](https://www.jpx.co.jp/equities/listing/disclosure/info/)）。**仅写公司名亦不豁免 → 两个版本均不点名 netstars。**
> 🔴 **M2ie（既有合作方）与 ChuanHouse（英文名未核实）在两版中均不出现。**

## X ／ 不点名版（★默认）｜274 字符

```
Five days. Tokyo to Osaka.

Nobody opened with "what can it do."

Every single time it was some version of: who else have you worked with, and for how long.

We come from a market where capability is the only question.

Duration is the harder one.

Nothing has been decided.
```
- `Nobody opened with "what can it do."` 是三条中最强的钩子：悬念前置，下一行揭晓。
- `Duration is the harder one.` 硬收，不解释——解释留给 LinkedIn 版。

## X ／ 点名版（⚠️ 需书面确认）｜256 字符

```
Five days. Tokyo to Osaka. The last meetings included NIKKOKU SOFT.

Nobody opened with "what can it do."

Every time: who else have you worked with, and for how long.

We come from a market where capability is the only question.

Nothing has been decided.
```
- `The last meetings included NIKKOKU SOFT.` 明示名单不完整，避免读者猜其余几家。
- NIKKOKU SOFT **官方英文法人表记未核实**，须按对方名片确认后替换。

## LinkedIn ／ 不点名版（★默认）｜247 词

```
Five days in Japan, Tokyo to Osaka, across industries with almost nothing in common. One question came up in every single room.

It was never "what can it do."

It was always some version of: who else have you worked with, and for how long.

We build AI development tools, and we come from a market where capability is the opening question and often the only one. Being asked about duration instead, five days running, across sectors that share no customers and no vocabulary, is the most useful thing we are carrying home.

The lazy reading is that this is a cultural preference to be accommodated. We think that is wrong, and a little condescending. It is an economically rational question. Switching costs are real and paid by the person asking. Reputations outlive the individual careers attached to them. A vendor who disappears in three years has cost you more than they ever saved you.

Read that way it is not a slower question. It is a more expensive one to answer honestly, which is exactly why fewer companies get asked it.

Ending the week in Osaka was deliberate. Treating Japan as one market with one centre was a shortcut we had been taking, and we will not take it again.

Nothing has been decided. We are grateful to everyone who gave us their time.

What does your company have to be able to say, five years from now, for that question to have a good answer?
```
- 🔑 **最关键段落**：`The lazy reading is that this is a cultural preference... We think that is wrong, and a little condescending.` 主动否认「文化差异」解读，改以**经济理性**归因，彻底堵住「日本决策慢」式隐含批评。这段是 X 版承载不了的，也是本条存在 LinkedIn 版的理由。
- `across industries with almost nothing in common` 刻意模糊：该组几乎全为华人背景在日企业，**绝不可按族裔分组呈现**。

## LinkedIn ／ 点名版（⚠️ 需书面确认）｜231 词

```
Five days in Japan, Tokyo to Osaka, across industries with almost nothing in common. The final meetings included NIKKOKU SOFT.

One question came up in every single room. It was never "what can it do."

It was always some version of: who else have you worked with, and for how long.

We build AI development tools, and we come from a market where capability is the opening question and often the only one. Being asked about duration instead, five days running, across sectors that share no customers and no vocabulary, is the most useful thing we are carrying home.

The lazy reading is that this is a cultural preference to be accommodated. We think that is wrong, and a little condescending. It is an economically rational question. Switching costs are real and paid by the person asking. Reputations outlive the individual careers attached to them. A vendor who disappears in three years has cost you more than they ever saved you.

Read that way it is not a slower question. It is a more expensive one to answer honestly, which is exactly why fewer companies get asked it.

Ending the week in Osaka was deliberate. Treating Japan as one market with one centre was a shortcut we had been taking, and we will not take it again.

Nothing has been decided. We are grateful to everyone who gave us their time.
```
- 删除结尾开放问题：点名版帖子下方的公开讨论会牵连被点名方，故收在致谢与否认句。
- 与 netstars **不构成直接竞品**（§25），风险纯来自上市开示，故不点名是合规选择而非竞品回避。

**⚠️ 加 hashtag 时的字符余量（X 版）**

`#JapanVisitNotes` 占 17 字符 + 换行。加后：x1a 291 / x1b 266 / x2a 293 / x2b 291 / x3a 292 / x3b 274。
→ **x1b 与 x3b 可直接加；其余四段加 tag 会超限**。若坚持全部带 tag，建议改用更短的 `#JapanNotes`（12 字符）或**仅在第一条加 tag**，后两条靠发布节奏形成系列。
